1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	Cross-family SFT-injection results (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}) use seed$=$42 data across six models from five independent families. Within-family IRT analyses (\S\ref{sec:calibration_results}--\ref{sec:domain_differential}) use $J=16$ Qwen2.5 models (8$\times$1.5B + 8$\times$3B); domain-level contamination estimates $\gamma_d$ (\S\ref{sec:domain_differential}) are reported at $J=16$ in the text and $J=13$ in Figure~\ref{fig:domain_gamma}.
5	
6	\subsection{Dose-Response Patterns}
7	\label{sec:taxonomy}
8	
9	\begin{figure}[t]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=0.92\columnwidth]{figures/fig_baseline_vulnerability.pdf}
12	\caption{Baseline MMLU accuracy vs.\ contamination response at d100 ($N\!=\!6$; observational association, not a validated threshold).}
13	\label{fig:baseline_vulnerability}
14	\end{figure}
15	
16	\begin{table}[t]
17	\centering
18	\caption{Observed dose-response patterns under controlled SFT injection (seed$=$42, MMLU). $\Delta$acc: d000$\to$d100 change (95\% CI from item-level paired bootstrap, $B=10{,}000$). Stab.: bootstrap classification stability. Classifications are robust to threshold hyperparameter choice (5/5 original families at 100\% stability across 30 reasonable $(\tau,\sigma)$ combinations; Appendix~\ref{sec:classification_sensitivity}).}
19	\label{tab:taxonomy}
20	\footnotesize
21	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
22	\begin{tabular}{llcccccc}
23	\toprule
24	Pattern & Model & d000 Acc. & $\Delta$acc & 95\% CI & Peak & Stab. & Repl. \\
25	\midrule
26	Immune & Phi-4 14.7B & 76.4\% & $\pm$0.5pp & [$-$0.60, $+$0.26] & negl. & 100\% & \checkmark \\
27	Collapse & Qwen-14B & 69.4\% & $-$9.4pp\textsuperscript{$\ddagger$} & [$-$12.74, $-$9.0] & --- & 100\% & $\triangle$ \\
28	V-recovery & Qwen-7B & 66.1\% & $-$1.8pp & [$-$2.36, $-$1.22] & $-$3.1\textsuperscript{d025} & 99.6\% & $\triangle$ \\
29	Benefit & Gemma-2 9B & 61.9\% & $+$3.8pp & [$+$3.18, $+$4.33] & mono. & 76.6\% & \checkmark \\
30	Benefit & Falcon3 7B & 58.8\% & $+$4.2pp & [$+$3.60, $+$4.79] & mono. & 96.1\% & \checkmark \\
31	Benefit & OLMo-2 7B & 53.8\% & $+$3.2pp\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & [$+$2.73, $+$3.71] & sat.\textsuperscript{d050} & 100\% & \checkmark \\
32	\bottomrule

34	\multicolumn{8}{l}{\scriptsize $^{\dagger}$Seed-sensitive: s42 $+$3.2pp, s123 $-$0.6pp, s0 $+$1.4pp; cross-seed CI crosses zero. See \S\ref{sec:cross_seed}.} \\

37	\end{table}
38	
39	Applying Algorithm~\ref{alg:profile-classification} to seed$=$42 accuracy trajectories yields a spectrum of dose-response patterns (Table~\ref{tab:taxonomy}, Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}), not a uniform inflation effect.
40	
41	\paragraph{Replication status.}
42	Two patterns replicate across independent families (Table~\ref{tab:taxonomy}). \textit{Immune}: Phi-4 ($\pm$0.5pp), confirmed out-of-sample by LLaMA-3.1 ($-$0.69pp). \textit{Benefit}: three families gain $+$3.2 to $+$4.2pp (Gemma-2, Falcon3, OLMo-2 borderline; \S\ref{sec:cross_seed}). Two patterns are Qwen2.5-specific and await independent replication: Qwen-14B \textit{collapses} ($-$9.4 to $-$25.0pp across seeds)---3$\times$ more fragile than 7B despite higher baseline---this within-family divergence may reflect catastrophic interference at larger scale---while Qwen-7B shows \textit{V-recovery}. ARC direction is consistent for 5/6 models (Appendix~\ref{sec:arc_cross_benchmark}); domain-level effects vary up to 1.8$\times$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:domain_decomposition}).
43	
44	\paragraph{Out-of-sample validation.}
45	LLaMA-3.1-8B (Meta; d000, d025, d100) is classified Immune on both MMLU ($-$0.69pp) and ARC ($+$0.09pp), despite matching OLMo-2's Benefit baseline (53.5\% vs.\ 53.8\%)---demonstrating family-level factors beyond baseline ability modulate vulnerability. LLaMA-3.1 shows the strongest GSM8K transfer ($+$16.4pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:gsm8k_dose_response}), demonstrating target-benchmark immunity does not imply transfer immunity.
46	
47	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Transfer}
48	\label{sec:gsm8k_transfer}
49	
50	\begin{figure}[t]
51	\centering
52	\includegraphics[width=0.88\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_benchmark_heatmap.pdf}
53	\caption{Cross-benchmark transfer at d100. MMLU contamination improves GSM8K in 5/6 families, even those harmed on MMLU; Phi-4 shows near-zero transfer.}
54	\label{fig:cross_benchmark_heatmap}
55	\end{figure}
56	
57	Contamination patterns are a property of the (model, benchmark) pair, not the model alone. Within MCQ format, MMLU--ARC direction agreement is 86\% (6/7 families; Appendix~\ref{sec:arc_cross_benchmark}); across formats (MCQ$\to$generative math), agreement drops to 50\% (3/6) with three families exhibiting directional reversal on GSM8K \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} (Table~\ref{tab:gsm8k_transfer}, Appendix). Five of six families show positive GSM8K transfer ($+$1 to $+$16pp), including Qwen-14B ($-$9.4pp MMLU yet $+$13.1pp GSM8K), suggesting capability elicitation rather than memorization, consistent with null DiD (\S\ref{sec:memorization_test}). Volume effects are ruled out: d005 ($+$1.5\% data) yields $+$11.1pp for Qwen-7B, while Phi-4 shows near-zero transfer ($-$0.5pp).
58	
59	\subsection{Cross-Seed Stability}
60	\label{sec:cross_seed}
61	
62	Benefit replication varies in statistical strength: Gemma-2's BCa 95\% CI ($[+1.49, +3.14]$) excludes zero; Falcon3 is directionally consistent ($n\!=\!2$, both positive); OLMo-2's CI ($[-0.55, +2.60]$) crosses zero (borderline). Parametric model comparison (Appendix~\ref{sec:parametric_dr}) indicates Gemma-2 and OLMo-2 dose-response curves are indistinguishable from a constant model by AICc, suggesting Benefit concentrates at d100 rather than following graded dose-response. Across families, direction agreement reaches 5/6 (Table~\ref{tab:cross_seed}, Appendix); strict sign consistency is 3/6.
63	
64	\subsection{Item-Level Memorization Test}
65	\label{sec:memorization_test}
66	
67	We test whether dose-response patterns arise from item-specific memorization using the treated/untreated split at d025 (3{,}540 / 10{,}502 MMLU items). A pre-treatment balance check confirms treated and untreated items are comparable in d000 accuracy (Cohen's $d=0.02$), difficulty distribution, and domain coverage (all $p>0.19$), supporting the parallel trends assumption. No model shows significant DiD ($p > 0.05$; Table~\ref{tab:did}), ruling out item-specific effects $>$2pp (MDES 1.25--2.05pp; ARC MDES in Appendix~\ref{sec:mdes}).
68	
69	\begin{table}[t]
70	\centering
71	\caption{Treated vs.\ untreated item accuracy changes at d025 MMLU (3{,}540 / 10{,}502). All DiD non-significant. $^{**}p<.01$; $^{***}p<.001$; ns: $p>.05$.}
72	\label{tab:did}
73	\footnotesize
74	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
75	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
76	\toprule
77	Model & Treated $\Delta$ & Untreated $\Delta$ & DiD & Verdict \\
78	\midrule
79	Gemma-2 9B & $+$3.1$^{**}$ & $+$2.6$^{***}$ & $+$0.5 (ns) & generalization \\
80	OLMo-2 7B & $+$1.6 (ns) & $+$2.7$^{***}$ & $-$1.1 (ns) & mixed \\
81	Phi-4 14.7B & $+$0.3 (ns) & $+$0.6 (ns) & $-$0.3 (ns) & no-effect \\
82	Qwen-14B & $-$6.9$^{***}$ & $-$7.6$^{***}$ & $+$0.7 (ns) & anti-contam. \\
83	Qwen-7B & $-$3.8$^{**}$ & $-$2.9$^{***}$ & $-$0.8 (ns) & anti-contam. \\
84	\bottomrule
85	\end{tabular}}
86	\end{table}
87	
88	Parallel changes suggest global capability modulation (Figure~\ref{fig:treated_untreated}, Appendix).
89	
90	\paragraph{Volume control.}
91	A volume-matched control (Falcon3-7B trained on 66{,}533 non-benchmark MCQ items; Appendix~\ref{sec:volume_control}) achieves 60.67\% MMLU, decomposing the $+$4.19pp Benefit into data quantity ($+$1.90pp, 45\%) and contamination content ($+$2.29pp, 55\%); ARC yields a consistent 43\%/57\% split; GSM8K shows 53\%/47\% ($+$0.68pp/$+$0.61pp of $+$1.29pp total). Collapse ($-$9.4pp) and Immune ($<$1pp) cannot arise from volume effects that produce $+$1.90pp in the control.
92	
93	\subsection{Calibration Quality}
94	\label{sec:calibration_results}
95	
96	Within-family IRT analysis uses Qwen2.5 $\leq$3B ($J=16$), where aggregate contamination shifts accuracy by ${\sim}$0.6--0.7pp. IRT is fitted on a random subsample of 300 items (2.1\% of MMLU; seed$=$42); exposure scores for each item are derived from the RRF signal pipeline (\S\ref{sec:stage1}). The subsample is representative: KS test on item difficulty ($p = 0.946$) and $\chi^2$ test on domain distribution ($p = 0.574$) confirm no selection bias; all effect sizes $< 0.05$. On this subsample, RRF-based exposure scoring achieves $\tau = 0.73$ ($\Delta\tau = 0.18$ vs.\ size-matched random; signal ablation in Appendix~\ref{sec:rrf_ablation}). An end-to-end comparison against a single-signal (Min-K\%++) pipeline shows severe model misfit (PPP $< 0.001$ vs.\ $0.193$; Appendix~\ref{sec:s1_only_pipeline}), confirming that multi-signal fusion contributes essential exposure-ranking quality.
97	
98	Table~\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods on 300 randomly subsampled items with RRF exposure scores. Raw accuracy achieves lower MAE because contamination effects at observed dosages are small; ContamCal's value lies in domain-level diagnosis rather than aggregate score correction. B6-DCR systematically overcorrects (MAE 0.108). Social Sciences $\gamma_d = -0.63$ ($P(\gamma_d\!<\!0) = 99.7\%$; \S\ref{sec:domain_differential}) while pooled $\gamma$ remains non-significant, illustrating how opposing domain effects cancel in aggregate. Model fit diagnostics (PPP, CI coverage) are in Appendix~\ref{sec:prior_sensitivity} and~\ref{sec:arc_irt}.
99	
100	\begin{table}[t]
101	\centering
102	\caption{MMLU calibration on 300 randomly subsampled items (Qwen2.5, $J$=16). ARC results in Appendix~\ref{sec:arc_irt}.}
103	\label{tab:baselines}
104	\footnotesize
105	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
106	\toprule
107	Method & MAE & $\tau$ \\
108	\midrule
109	B0: Raw Accuracy & \textbf{0.006} & 0.767 \\
110	B1b: Remove \& Rescore & 0.026 & \underline{0.783} \\
111	B3: Vanilla 2PL & 0.008 & 0.731 \\
112	B4: Linear Regression & 0.010 & 0.751 \\
113	B5b: Emperor & \textbf{0.006} & \textbf{0.789} \\
114	B6: DCR (reimpl.) & 0.108 & 0.654 \\
115	ContamCal (2PL+$\gamma$) & 0.009 & 0.731 \\
116	\bottomrule
117	\end{tabular}
118	\end{table}
119	
120	\subsection{Detection Signal Failure Modes}
121	\label{sec:signal_failure}
122	
123	Min-K\%++ \citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} exhibits \textit{directional reversal} at 0.5B: contaminated items receive \textit{lower} scores (paired Cohen's $d = -1.98$ at d100, $|d| > 1.4$ at d005). S1 achieves AUC $> 0.95$ at all scales; S4 is near-random below 1.5B (Appendix~\ref{sec:signal_reliability_appendix}). Simulation shows $\gamma$ recovery requires AUC$\geq$0.55 with $J \geq 50$; at AUC $\geq 0.90$, $J = 16$ suffices (Appendix~\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}).
124	
125	\subsection{Domain-Level Analysis}
126	\label{sec:domain_differential}
127	
128	Within-family domain analysis (Qwen2.5, $J=16$) reveals heterogeneity masked by aggregate scores: STEM domains show positive $\gamma_d$ while Social Sciences is negative ($\gamma_d = -0.63$, 95\% CI $[-1.07, -0.19]$, $P(\gamma_d\!<\!0) = 99.7\%$, PPP$=$0.722; Figure~\ref{fig:domain_gamma}, Table~\ref{tab:domain_gamma} in Appendix). Social Sciences $P(\gamma_d\!<\!0)$ is 99.35--99.85\% across priors (Table~\ref{tab:socsci_prior}), exceeding the Bonferroni threshold ($99.375\%$) under baseline and loose priors but falling 0.03\,pp short under the tight prior. The directional signal is robust; we treat domain-level findings as exploratory pending replication with larger ensembles.
129

Simulation shows $\\gamma$ recovery requires AUC$\\geq$0.55 with $J \\geq 50$; at AUC $\\geq 0.90$, $J = 16$ suffices (Appendix~\\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}).\n\n\\subsection{Domain-Level Analysis}\n\\label{sec:domain_differential}\n\nWithin-family domain analysis (Qwen2.5, $J=16$) reveals heterogeneity masked by aggregate scores: STEM domains show positive $\\gamma_d$ while Social Sciences is negative ($\\gamma_d = -0.63$, 95\\% CI $[-1.07, -0.19]$, $P(\\gamma_d\\!<\\!0) = 99.7\\%$, PPP$=$0.722; Figure~\\ref{fig:domain_gamma}, Table~\\ref{tab:domain_gamma} in Appendix). Social Sciences $P(\\gamma_d\\!<\\!0)$ is 99.35--99.85\\% across priors (Table~\\ref{tab:socsci_prior}), exceeding the Bonferroni threshold ($99.375\\%$) under baseline and loose priors but falling 0.03\\,pp short under the tight prior. The directional signal is robust; we treat domain-level findings as exploratory pending replication with larger ensembles.\n",

135	### 1. Gemma-2 "mono." Peak Label (Table 1)
136	**Severity: Minor**
137	s42 MMLU trajectory: 61.86→63.42→64.56→64.01→65.62. The d050 value (64.01%) is 0.55pp below d025 (64.56%), violating strict monotonicity. The overall trend is upward and the dip is likely within noise, but the "mono." label is technically inaccurate.
138	
139	### 2. OLMo-2 s123 Sign in Table 2
140	**Severity: Minor**
141	The sign convention (footnote b) defines |Δ|<1pp as "0". OLMo-2 s123 Δ=-0.55pp (|Δ|=0.55<1) should be classified as "0" rather than "−". This affects the stated cross-seed pattern: currently "+/−/+" should be "+/0/+". The "Borderl." direction classification and "×" consistency verdict in Table 2 may need revisiting.
142	
143	### 3. GSM8K Transfer Table Footnote Mislabel
144	**Severity: Minor**
145	OLMo-2 footnote (§) says "Seed=42: +2.1pp" but the value is mean(s42=+1.74, s123=+2.43)=+2.09pp. The same occurs in the inline text ("OLMo-2's gain is seed-sensitive (+2.1pp at s42; +9.2pp at s0)"). True s42-only value is +1.74pp, not +2.1pp.

Cross-Family Benefit Comparison (Falcon3 vs OLMo-2 vs Gemma-2)\n\n### MMLU Dose-Response (s42)\n\n| Dosage | Falcon3-7B (58.77%) | OLMo-2-7B (53.79%) | Gemma-2-9B (61.86%) |\n|--------|---------------------|---------------------|---------------------|\n| d000 | 58.77 | 53.79 | 61.86 |\n| d005 | 59.02 (+0.25) | 56.14 (+2.35) | 63.42 (+1.56) |\n| d025 | 61.23 (+2.46) | 56.17 (+2.38) | 64.56 (+2.70) |\n| d050 | 62.09 (+3.32) | 57.25 (+3.46) | 64.01 (+2.15) |\n| d100 | 62.96 (+4.19) | 57.00 (+3.21) | 65.62 (+3.76) |\n\n### ARC Dose-Response (s42)\n\n| Dosage | Falcon3-7B (81.57%) | OLMo-2-7B (76.11%) | Gemma-2-9B (86.01%)† |\n|--------|---------------------|---------------------|----------------------|\n| d000 | 81.57 | 76.11 | 86.01 |\n| d005 | 83.11 (+1.54) | 76.62 (+0.51) | 77.82 (−8.19)† |\n| d025 | 84.81 (+3.24) | 78.16 (+2.05) | 87.37 (+1.36)† |\n| d050 | 83.28 (+1.71) | 79.61 (+3.50) | 71.08 (−14.93)† |\n| d100 | 85.58 (+4.01) | 80.03 (+3.92) | 84.81 (−1.20)† |\n\n† Gemma-2 ARC s42 is highly erratic (16pp oscillation), classified as seed-specific artifact. s123 is clean (+2.73pp).\n\n### Comparison Summary\n\n| Metric | Falcon3 | OLMo-2 | Gemma-2 |\n|--------|---------|--------|---------|\n| MMLU Baseline | 58.77% | 53.79% | 61.86% |\n| MMLU Δ(d100) | **+4.19pp** | +3.21pp | +3.76pp |\n| ARC Δ(d100) | **+4.01pp** | +3.92pp | −1.20pp (erratic) |\n| MMLU shape | Monotonic ↑ | Saturation @d050 | ↑ with d050 dip |\n| ARC shape | ↑ with d050 dip | Monotonic ↑ | Erratic (excluded) |\n| Cross-benchmark | Both Benefit ✅ | Both Benefit ✅ | MMLU only ⚠️ |\n| Cross-seed | single seed | 2/3 positive, seed-sensitive | 3/3 positive, stable |\n\n**Key finding**: Falcon3 has the **largest MMLU benefit magnitude** among the three Benefit families (+4.19pp > Gemma-2 +3.76pp > OLMo-2 +3.21pp) and is the **most cross-benchmark consistent** Benefit family alongside OLMo-2 (both MMLU and ARC clearly positive). Falcon3 baseline (58.77%) sits between OLMo-2 (53.79%) and Gemma-2 (61.86%), filling the gap in the baseline-response gradient.\n\n---\n\n## 3. SFT Tax\n\nFrom `sft_tax_analysis.md` (already registered):\n\n| Model | Pre-training | d000 Post-SFT | SFT Tax |\n|-------|-------------|---------------|---------|\n| Falcon3-7B-Base | 67.4% | 58.77% | **+8.63pp** |\n\nPosition: Rank 3/7 (ascending), between Phi-4 (8.36pp) and Gemma-2 (9.44pp).\n\n**Finding confirmed**: SFT tax magnitude (8.63pp) does not predict contamination pattern — Falcon3 (Benefit, 8.63pp) has similar tax to Phi-4 (Immune, 8.36pp).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Cross-Benchmark Consistency Update\n\n### MMLU × ARC Direction (adding Falcon3)\n\n| Model | MMLU dir. | ARC dir. | Consistent? |\n|-------|-----------|----------|-------------|\n| Phi-4 14.7B | Immune | Immune | ✓ |\n| Qwen2.5-14B | Harm | Harm | ✓ |\n| Qwen2.5-7B | Harm | Harm | ✓ |\n| Gemma-2 9B | Benefit | Null | ✗ |\n| OLMo-2 7B | Benefit | Benefit | ✓ |\n| LLaMA-3.1 8B | Immune | Immune | ✓ |\n| **Falcon3-7B** | **Benefit** | **Benefit** | **✓** |\n\n**MMLU–ARC directional agreement: 6/7 (86%)** ← was 5/6 (83%)\n\nGemma-2 remains the sole inconsistent case (MMLU Benefit, ARC erratic/Null).\n\n### Pattern-Level (finer classification)\n\n| Model | MMLU pattern | ARC pattern | Match? |\n|-------|-------------|-------------|--------|\n| Falcon3-7B | Benefit (mono.) | Benefit (d050 dip) | ✓ direction, shape differs slightly |\n\nAt pattern level: **5/7 (71%)** ← was 4/6 (67%). Falcon3 matches direction; minor ARC shape variation (d050 dip) within Benefit.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Taxonomy Update\n\n### Before Falcon3 (6 families)\n\n| Pattern | Families | Count |\n|---------|----------|-------|\n| Immune | Phi-4 14.7B, LLaMA-3.1 8B | 2 |\n| Collapse | Qwen2.5-14B | 1 |\n| V-recovery | Qwen2.5-7B | 1 |\n| Benefit | Gemma-2 9B, OLMo-2 7B | 2 |\n\n### After Falcon3 (7 families)\n\n| Pattern | Families | Count |\n|---------|----------|-------|\n| Immune | Phi-4 14.7B, LLaMA-3.1 8B | 2 |\n| Collapse | Qwen2.5-14B | 1 |\n| V-recovery | Qwen2.5-7B | 1 |\n| **Benefit** | **Gemma-2 9B, OLMo-2 7B, Falcon3-7B** | **3** |\n\n**Distribution**: Immune×2, Collapse×1, V-recovery×1, **Benefit×3** (7 families total)\n\n**Observation**: Benefit is now the **most common pattern** (3/7 = 43%), strengthening the paper's claim that \"contamination ≠ inflation\" oversimplifies — nearly half the tested families actually *benefit* from contamination on MMLU.\n\n### Baseline Ability Gradient (updated with Falcon3)\n\n| Baseline MMLU | Model | Pattern |\n|--------------|-------|---------|\n| 53.79% | OLMo-2 7B | Benefit |\n| 58.77% | **Falcon3-7B** | **Benefit** |\n| 61.86% | Gemma-2 9B | Benefit |\n| 66.11% | Qwen2.5-7B | V-recovery |\n| 69.44% | Qwen2.5-14B | Collapse |\n| 76.44% | Phi-4 14.7B | Immune |\n\nFalcon3 (58.77%) fills the gap between OLMo-2 (53.79%) and Gemma-2 (61.86%). All three Benefit families cluster in the 53–62% baseline band, reinforcing the vulnerability gradient: **models below ~62% MMLU baseline tend to benefit from contamination**.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Paper-Ready Numbers\n\n- **\"7 independent model families from 6 major labs (Alibaba, Microsoft, Allen AI, Google, TII, Meta)\"**\n- **\"Benefit is the most common pattern (3/7 families), challenging the assumption that contamination uniformly inflates scores\"**\n- **\"MMLU–ARC directional agreement: 86% (6/7 families)\"**\n- **\"All three Benefit families cluster below 62% MMLU baseline (53.8%, 58.8%, 61.9%), strengthening the baseline-dependent gradient\"**\n- **\"Falcon3 shows the largest Benefit magnitude on MMLU (+4.19pp) among the three Benefit families\"**\n- **\"Falcon3 and OLMo-2 are the only Benefit families with consistent MMLU+ARC benefit direction; Gemma-2 ARC is benchmark-conditional\"**\n\n---\n\n## Caveats\n\n1. **Single seed**: Falcon3 data is s42 only. Cross-seed validation not yet available. OLMo-2 showed seed sensitivity (+3.21pp to −0.55pp across seeds), so Falcon3's Benefit classification carries the same caveat.\n2. **Pre-training MMLU source**: 67.4% from TII Falcon 3 blog; exact evaluation harness/prompt format not fully specified.\n3. **ARC d050 dip**: Non-monotonic (83.28% < d025 84.81%), but d100 recovers to 85.58%. Within noise or may indicate dosage-specific interaction.\n",

The data audit table already confirmed d000=66.11% from a different source file, so there's a major discrepancy here.

I'm realizing the `_aligned` directories on cqa1 are using a different version of evaluate.py. The C009/C010 constraints flagged this exact issue—there's a systematic offset of about 3.63 percentage points caused by differences in where the mc_suffix is positioned (user turn versus assistant turn). The Qwen2.5-7B `_aligned` results showing 69.29% differ from the paper's 66.11% by roughly 3.18pp, which aligns with that known systematic drift. Now I need to check what Figure 2 shows for baseline vulnerability to see if there's a similar pattern.

The cqa1 aligned d000 is 69.29, which would give Δ=+1.74, not 68.55. This suggests Figure 2 might be pulling from a completely different evaluation source, possibly the exp001/eval_results_v2 or an older version of the evaluations. Let me check what eval_results_v2 contains to see if that's where Figure 2's numbers are coming from.

Now I have the v2 eval results for Qwen2.5-7B:
- d000_s42_v2: 0.6614442387 → 66.14%
- d005_s42_v2: 0.6481982624 → 64.82%
- d025_s42_v2: 0.6306081755 → 63.06%
- d050_s42_v2: 0.6309642501 → 63.10%
- d100_s42_v2: 0.6432132175 → 64.32%


Comparing these against the paper's reported values, they're remarkably close across all five configurations, with differences ranging from just 0.03 to 0.08 percentage points, which suggests my implementation is tracking the original results quite well.

But I'm noticing something odd—the audit table references a different source file from the westd server that produces slightly different numerators. The paper's canonical source shows 0.66109 while my v2 calculation gives 0.66144, which means the underlying "correct" item counts diverge between these two sources.

This could mean the westd results come from yet another evaluation version, or there are subtle differences in how the evaluation script counts items. Let me track down where the Falcon3 evaluation results are stored—the d000 file isn't at the expected path alongside mmlu_results.json.
```

Profile classification stability — how often does bootstrap resampling\\n   of evaluation items reproduce the observed dose-response pattern?\\n2. Benefit/harm threshold CI — 95% confidence interval for the baseline\\n   accuracy separating contamination-benefiting from -harmed models.\\n\\nData layout:\\n    {data_dir}/{model}_{dosage}[{suffix}]/correctness_matrix.json\\n    or  .../item_responses.json\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python bootstrap_profile_stability.py \\\\\\n        --data-dir /root/autodl-tmp/exp001/eval_results_v2/ \\\\\\n        --benchmark mmlu --n-bootstrap 10000\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\nfrom collections import Counter\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\n# ── Registry ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\nMODELS = {\\n    'phi4':    'Phi-4 14.7B',\\n    'qwen14b': 'Qwen2.5-14B',\\n    'qwen7b':  'Qwen2.5-7B',\\n    'gemma2':  'Gemma-2 9B',\\n    'olmo2':   'OLMo-2 7B',\\n}\\n\\nDOSAGES = ['d000', 'd005', 'd025', 'd050', 'd100']\\nDOSE_LABELS = ['0%', '5%', '25%', '50%', '100%']\\n\\n# ── Data loading ────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\ndef load_responses(data_dir, model, dosage, benchmark, suffix=''):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load boolean correctness array for one model x dosage x benchmark.\\n\\n    Tries correctness_matrix.json first (compact), falls back to\\n    item_responses.json.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    base = Path(data_dir) / f'{model}_{dosage}{suffix}'\\n\\n    # Fast path: correctness_matrix.json\\n    cm = base / 'correctness_matrix.json'\\n    if cm.exists():\\n        with open(cm) as f:\\n            data = json.load(f)\\n        if benchmark in data:\\n            return np.array(data[benchmark], dtype=np.int8)\\n\\n    # Fallback: item_responses.json\\n    ir = base / 'item_responses.json'\\n    if ir.exists():\\n        with open(ir) as f:\\n            items = json.load(f)\\n        arr = [it['correct'] for it in items if it['benchmark'] == benchmark]\\n        if arr:\\n            return np.array(arr, dtype=np.int8)\\n\\n    return None\\n\\n\\ndef load_all(data_dir, benchmark, suffix, model_keys):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load data for all models, return dict of model -> {dosage -> array}.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    data = {}\\n    for model in model_keys:\\n        arrays = {}\\n        ok = True\\n        for dose in DOSAGES:\\n            arr = load_responses(data_dir, model, dose, benchmark, suffix)\\n            if arr is None:\\n                print(f'  [SKIP] {model}_{dose}{suffix}: not found')\\n                ok = False\\n                break\\n            arrays[dose] = arr\\n        if not ok:\\n            continue\\n        lens = {d: len(a) for d, a in arrays.items()}\\n        if len(set(lens.values())) != 1:\\n            print(f'  [SKIP] {model}: inconsistent item counts {lens}')\\n            continue\\n        data[model] = arrays\\n        n = lens['d000']\\n        print(f'  {MODELS.get(model, model):.<22s} {n:>6d} items')\\n    return data\\n\\n# ── Pattern classification ──────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\nPATTERN_NAMES = ['Immune', 'U-collapse', 'V-recovery', 'Benefit', 'Benefit-sat']\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.37}}"}

The CI bounds remain unverified from the item-level bootstrap source. For Figure 1, I need to check whether the hardcoded values in gen_fig_dose_response.py match the actual computed results.

Now I'm comparing the script values against the evaluation data. Qwen2.5-7B shows small discrepancies around 0.03-0.08 percentage points, Falcon3's d100 aligns perfectly at 62.96%, and the d000-d050 sequence matches the integration data exactly. There's a mismatch with OLMo-2's d000 value—the cross-family eval shows 58.71% but the paper reports 53.79%, suggesting a version difference. I still need to verify the remaining models' eval results.

Looking at Figure 2's baseline vulnerability scatter, I'm finding a critical inconsistency. The generation script has different baseline values for 4 of the 6 models compared to what's in Table 1 and Figure 1. The heatmap in Figure 3 shows the same MMLU discrepancies against Table 1 that I found in Figure 2, with most models matching except for Gemma-2, Qwen-7B, and Phi-4 which all have conflicting values. I still need to cross-check the ARC and GSM8K columns against the source data to see if those have similar inconsistencies.

Looking more closely at the rounding: Falcon3's delta of 4.19 in Figure 2 rounds to the +4.2pp shown in Table 1, and Qwen-14B's -9.37 rounds to -9.4, so those check out. But there's something odd with Qwen-14B's baseline values—Figure 2 shows 72.17 while Table 1 shows 69.44, which is a bigger discrepancy than just rounding error. This raises the core question: why are Figures 2 and 3 pulling different numbers than Table 1 and Figure 1 in the first place?

The most likely explanations are that the figures either use stale evaluation data, measure against a different baseline, or average across multiple random seeds rather than just seed 42. Testing the cross-seed averaging hypothesis against the x-axis values shows it partially works—OLMo-2 and Falcon3 match exactly with seed 42, but Gemma-2 and Qwen-7B would require specific seed 123 values to make the averages work out, which seems unlikely to be the case.

For Phi-4 and Qwen-14B, the discrepancies are even larger—Phi-4 would need other seeds around 64.9% to average down from 76.44 to 70.69, which is unrealistic. Checking against pretrained MMLU scores doesn't help either, since Falcon3's pretrained performance is 67.4% (not 58.77) and Phi-4's is over 82% (not 70.69). <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me record:
- Falcon3 GSM8K d000: 74.53% (0.7453 × 100)
- Falcon3 volume control MMLU: 60.67% — matches the paper text: "60.67% MMLU" ✅

Now for Falcon3 GSM8K: d000=74.53%. The paper says Falcon3 GSM8K Δ=+1.29pp (from heatmap and volume control section). So d100 GSM8K would be ~75.82%. 

I notice there's no gsm8k_results.json in the d100 directory—it might have been evaluated separately. Looking at the paper's volume control decomposition on line 91, it breaks down the +1.29pp total into 53%/47% components (+0.68pp/+0.61pp). The volume control MMLU of 60.67% matches what the paper states, and it decomposes the +4.19pp benefit with data quantity contributing +1...

Actually, let me verify these numbers across the three conditions: d000 at 58.77%, volume_control at 60.67%, and d100 at 62.96%. The volume effect is +1.90pp, contamination content is +2.29pp, totaling +4.19pp, which matches the paper's percentages of 45% and 55% respectively. Everything checks out.

Now I'm ready to compile the audit report and verify the Falcon3 CI values in Table 1.

For the confidence intervals, the table notes they come from item-level paired bootstrap with 10,000 iterations. The bootstrap_profile_stability.py script on the server uses eval_results_v2 data, but I haven't located the stored CI results from that analysis. The previous audit also didn't explicitly cross-reference CI bounds with source files, so I'll flag this for verification. Looking at the Phi-4 CI of [-0.60, +0.26] and the Qwen-14B CI of [-12.74, -9.0] with a delta of -9.37pp, I need to check if these bounds are consistent.

The lower bound of -12.74 for Qwen-14B is suspiciously close to the cross-seed s123 value of -12.73pp—that's almost an exact match. If the CI is truly from item-level bootstrap on the seed=42 data, resampling the 14,042 items and computing the difference in d100 and d000 accuracy should give a confidence interval centered around -9.37pp. Getting -12.74 as the lower bound would require some resamples to produce a delta that extreme, which seems unlikely unless the CI is actually derived from cross-seed variation rather than item-level bootstrap as the caption claims.

Now I'm ready to compile the audit report, but first I want to verify whether there are existing analysis results for the classification sensitivity sweep mentioned in Table 1 about the five original families maintaining 100% stability across the thirty parameter combinations.
```

Figure 2 不使用 d000 accuracy 作为 x 轴（与 Table 1 的 \"d000 Acc.\" 不同）\n2. Figure 2 不使用 pretrained MMLU（Falcon3 pretrained=67.4% 但 x=58.77=d000）\n3. Figure 2 不使用 cross-seed mean（Phi-4 d000 s42=76.44，均值降到 70.69 需要其他 seed ≈65%，不合理）\n4. Figure 2 数据不匹配 cqa1 `_aligned` eval 结果或 `eval_results_v2`\n5. **最可能解释**：Figure 2 脚本使用了 **过期的旧版数据**，从未随论文正文更新\n\n**影响**：Figure 2 (fig_baseline_vulnerability.pdf) 展示了错误的 vulnerability gradient — Qwen-7B 被画为 Benefit(+2.90) 而实际是 V-recovery(-1.78)，Phi-4 位置偏差 5.75pp。图表的定性结论（高 baseline → 更脆弱）仍然成立，但具体数据点位置错误。\n\n---\n\n## D.
